- [ ] **Step 3: Write the tests for the uncovered paths**

Four job-scoped paths have no test in any other task: restart, approve,
artifact download and MCP `get_artifact`. Each test checks two things. A
pinned job whose own `task_folder` is denied must be denied, even though a
task of the same name sits in an ALLOWED folder on `main`. And the denial must
come from the ACL (`"Job not found"`), not from the path's own later check:
the admin control shows that check answering differently.

Create `crates/stroem-server/tests/read_path_audit_test.rs`:

```rust
//! § 7.9 read-path audit — the job-scoped paths not tested elsewhere
//! (restart, approve, artifact download, MCP `get_artifact`). Every other
//! job-scoped path is tested in `git_refs_read_paths_test.rs` (Part E1); see
//! the audit table in the spec.

mod common;
use common::pinned::*;

use anyhow::Result;
use axum::body::Body;
use axum::http::{Request, StatusCode};
use http_body_util::BodyExt;
use serde_json::{json, Value as JsonValue};
use stroem_server::config::{AclAction, AclConfig, AclRule};
use tower::ServiceExt;
use uuid::Uuid;

const ALICE: &str = "alice@test.local"; // Run on live/* only
const ADMIN: &str = "admin@test.local";

/// Live `nightly` is in folder `live` (allowed for alice); the trigger pins it to release/2.3.
const AUDIT_ETL_MAIN: &str = r#"
actions:
  noop:
    type: script
    script: "echo main"
tasks:
  nightly:
    folder: live
    flow:
      a:
        action: noop
triggers:
  nightly-at-ref:
    type: scheduler
    cron: "0 0 1 1 *"
    task: nightly
    ref: release/2.3
"#;

/// The same task name at release/2.3 declares folder `rel` (denied for alice).
const AUDIT_ETL_RELEASE: &str = r#"
actions:
  noop:
    type: script
    script: "echo release"
tasks:
  nightly:
    folder: rel
    flow:
      a:
        action: noop
"#;

struct Audit {
    fx: PinnedFixture,
    alice: String,
    admin: String,
    /// Scheduler-created pinned job of `nightly@release/2.3` (task_folder `rel`).
    job: Uuid,
}

async fn audit_setup() -> Result<Audit> {
    let fx = pinned_workspace_fixture(PinnedFixtureOpts {
        acl: Some(AclConfig {
            default: AclAction::Deny,
            rules: vec![AclRule {
                workspace: "*".into(),
                tasks: vec!["live/*".into()],
                action: AclAction::Run,
                groups: vec![],
                users: vec![ALICE.into()],
            }],
        }),
        users: vec![
            FixtureUser { email: ALICE, groups: vec![], admin: false },
            FixtureUser { email: ADMIN, groups: vec![], admin: true },
        ],
        mcp: true,
        etl_main: Some(AUDIT_ETL_MAIN.to_string()),
        etl_release: Some(AUDIT_ETL_RELEASE.to_string()),
        ..Default::default()
    })
    .await?;
    let alice = fx.api_key(ALICE, false).await;
    let admin = fx.api_key(ADMIN, true).await;

    stroem_server::scheduler::fire_trigger_once(&fx.state, fx.mgr(), fx.mgr(), "etl/nightly-at-ref")
        .await;
    let job: Uuid = sqlx::query_scalar(
        "SELECT job_id FROM job WHERE source_type = 'trigger' AND source_id = 'etl/nightly-at-ref'",
    )
    .fetch_one(&fx.pool)
    .await?;
    let row = stroem_db::JobRepo::get(&fx.pool, job).await?.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(row.git_ref.as_deref(), Some("release/2.3"));
    assert_eq!(row.task_folder.as_deref(), Some("rel"), "pinned folder stamped");
    Ok(Audit { fx, alice, admin, job })
}

#[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread")]
async fn restart_denied() -> Result<()> {
    let a = audit_setup().await?;
    let uri = format!("/api/jobs/{}/restart", a.job);
    let body = json!({"from_step": "a", "dry_run": true});
    let (st, resp) = api_req(&a.fx.router, "POST", &uri, Some(&a.alice), Some(body.clone())).await;
    assert_eq!(st, StatusCode::NOT_FOUND, "{resp}");
    assert_eq!(resp["error"], "Job not found", "{resp}");
    // Control: the admin passes the ACL and reaches the terminal-state check.
    let (st, resp) = api_req(&a.fx.router, "POST", &uri, Some(&a.admin), Some(body)).await;
    assert_eq!(st, StatusCode::CONFLICT, "the pinned job is still running: {resp}");
    Ok(())
}

#[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread")]
async fn approve_denied() -> Result<()> {
    let a = audit_setup().await?;
    let uri = format!("/api/jobs/{}/steps/a/approve", a.job);
    let body = json!({"approved": true});
    let (st, resp) = api_req(&a.fx.router, "POST", &uri, Some(&a.alice), Some(body.clone())).await;
    assert_eq!(st, StatusCode::NOT_FOUND, "{resp}");
    assert_eq!(resp["error"], "Job not found", "{resp}");
    // Control: the admin passes the ACL; `a` is not a suspended approval step.
    let (st, resp) = api_req(&a.fx.router, "POST", &uri, Some(&a.admin), Some(body)).await;
    assert_ne!(resp["error"], "Job not found", "{st}: {resp}");
    Ok(())
}

#[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread")]
async fn artifact_download_denied() -> Result<()> {
    let a = audit_setup().await?;
    let uri = format!("/api/jobs/{}/artifacts/report.txt", a.job);
    let (st, resp) = api_req(&a.fx.router, "GET", &uri, Some(&a.alice), None).await;
    assert_eq!(st, StatusCode::NOT_FOUND);
    assert_eq!(resp["error"], "Job not found", "the ACL answered, not the artifact lookup: {resp}");
    // Control: the admin passes the ACL and gets the artifact lookup's own 404.
    let (st, resp) = api_req(&a.fx.router, "GET", &uri, Some(&a.admin), None).await;
    assert_eq!(st, StatusCode::NOT_FOUND);
    assert_ne!(resp["error"], "Job not found", "{resp}");
    Ok(())
}

/// One MCP tool call over Streamable HTTP (initialize + tools/call), with an API key.
async fn audit_mcp_call(router: &axum::Router, token: &str, tool: &str, args: JsonValue) -> Result<JsonValue> {
    let req = |id: u64, method: &str, params: JsonValue, session: Option<&str>| {
        let mut b = Request::builder()
            .method("POST")
            .uri("/mcp")
            .header("Host", "localhost")
            .header("Content-Type", "application/json")
            .header("Accept", "application/json, text/event-stream")
            .header("Authorization", format!("Bearer {token}"));
        if let Some(s) = session {
            b = b.header("Mcp-Session-Id", s);
        }
        b.body(Body::from(
            json!({"jsonrpc": "2.0", "id": id, "method": method, "params": params}).to_string(),
        ))
        .unwrap()
    };
    let init = router
        .clone()
        .oneshot(req(
            0,
            "initialize",
            json!({"protocolVersion": "2025-03-26", "capabilities": {}, "clientInfo": {"name": "audit", "version": "1"}}),
            None,
        ))
        .await?;
    let session = init
        .headers()
        .get("Mcp-Session-Id")
        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
        .map(str::to_string);
    let resp = router
        .clone()
        .oneshot(req(
            1,
            "tools/call",
            json!({"name": tool, "arguments": args}),
            session.as_deref(),
        ))
        .await?;
    assert_eq!(resp.status(), StatusCode::OK);
    Ok(serde_json::from_slice(&resp.into_body().collect().await?.to_bytes())?)
}

#[tokio::test(flavor = "multi_thread")]
async fn mcp_get_artifact_denied() -> Result<()> {
    let a = audit_setup().await?;
    let args = json!({"job_id": a.job.to_string(), "name": "report.txt"});
    let denied = audit_mcp_call(&a.fx.router, &a.alice, "get_artifact", args.clone()).await?;
    assert!(
        denied.get("error").is_some() || denied["result"]["isError"].as_bool().unwrap_or(false),
        "{denied}"
    );
    assert!(denied.to_string().contains("Job not found"), "the ACL answered: {denied}");
    // Control: the admin passes the ACL and reaches the artifact lookup.
    let admin = audit_mcp_call(&a.fx.router, &a.admin, "get_artifact", args).await?;
    assert!(!admin.to_string().contains("Job not found"), "{admin}");
    Ok(())
}
```

# --- 0b. Git workspace for the `ref:` tests (spec 2026-10-02) ---
# A bare repo with main, a `release/1` branch and a `v1.0.0` tag. Each ref
# carries its own data/version.txt, so the outputs prove which commit ran.
REFS_DIR="$PROJECT_DIR/tests/.e2e-refs"
rm -rf "$REFS_DIR"
mkdir -p "$REFS_DIR"
REFS_WORK="$REFS_DIR/work"
git init -q -b main "$REFS_WORK"
git -C "$REFS_WORK" config user.email e2e@stroem.local
git -C "$REFS_WORK" config user.name e2e
mkdir -p "$REFS_WORK/.workflows" "$REFS_WORK/data"
cat > "$REFS_WORK/.workflows/refs.yaml" <<'YAML'
actions:
  which-version:
    type: script
    runner: local
    script: |
      echo "VERSION=$(cat data/version.txt)"
      echo "OUTPUT: {\"version\": \"$(cat data/version.txt)\"}"
  run-version-task:
    type: task
    task: version-task
    ref: release/1

tasks:
  version-task:
    mode: distributed
    flow:
      v:
        action: which-version
  release-matrix:
    mode: distributed
    flow:
      from-main:
        action: which-version
      from-branch:
        action: which-version
        ref: release/1
      from-tag:
        action: which-version
        ref: v1.0.0
      child:
        action: run-version-task
YAML
echo "main" > "$REFS_WORK/data/version.txt"
git -C "$REFS_WORK" add -A && git -C "$REFS_WORK" commit -q -m "main"
git -C "$REFS_WORK" checkout -q -b release/1
echo "tag-v1" > "$REFS_WORK/data/version.txt"
git -C "$REFS_WORK" commit -qam "v1.0.0" && git -C "$REFS_WORK" tag v1.0.0
echo "branch-r1" > "$REFS_WORK/data/version.txt"
git -C "$REFS_WORK" commit -qam "release/1 hotfix"
REFS_R1_SHA=$(git -C "$REFS_WORK" rev-parse release/1)
REFS_TAG_SHA=$(git -C "$REFS_WORK" rev-parse 'v1.0.0^{commit}')
git -C "$REFS_WORK" checkout -q main
git clone -q --bare "$REFS_WORK" "$REFS_DIR/refs-ws.git"

REFS_OVERRIDE="$REFS_DIR/compose.override.yml"
cat > "$REFS_OVERRIDE" <<YAML
services:
  server:
    volumes:
      - $REFS_DIR/refs-ws.git:/git/refs-ws.git
    environment:
      STROEM__WORKSPACES__REFS__TYPE: git
      STROEM__WORKSPACES__REFS__URL: file:///git/refs-ws.git
      STROEM__WORKSPACES__REFS__REF: main
YAML
COMPOSE="$COMPOSE -f $REFS_OVERRIDE"

```bash
# --- 20. Git refs: action + branch ref, action + tag ref, type: task + ref ---
info "Triggering release-matrix (git workspace 'refs', ref: on steps and a task action)..."
EXEC_RESP_RM=$(acurl -X POST "$BASE_URL/api/workspaces/refs/tasks/release-matrix/execute" \
    -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"input": {}}')
RM_JOB_ID=$(echo "$EXEC_RESP_RM" | jq -r '.job_id')
[ -n "$RM_JOB_ID" ] && [ "$RM_JOB_ID" != "null" ] || fail "release-matrix execute failed: $EXEC_RESP_RM"
RM_POLLED=0; RM_STATUS="pending"
while [ "$RM_STATUS" != "completed" ] && [ "$RM_STATUS" != "failed" ]; do
    sleep 2; RM_POLLED=$((RM_POLLED + 2))
    [ "$RM_POLLED" -lt "$MAX_POLL" ] || { acurl "$BASE_URL/api/jobs/$RM_JOB_ID" | jq .; fail "release-matrix did not finish"; }
    RM_DETAIL=$(acurl "$BASE_URL/api/jobs/$RM_JOB_ID"); RM_STATUS=$(echo "$RM_DETAIL" | jq -r '.status'); printf "."
done; echo ""
[ "$RM_STATUS" = "completed" ] || { echo "$RM_DETAIL" | jq .; fail "release-matrix failed"; }
pass "release-matrix completed"

rm_out() { echo "$RM_DETAIL" | jq -r --arg s "$1" '.steps[] | select(.step_name==$s) | .output.version'; }
[ "$(rm_out from-main)" = "main" ] || fail "from-main ran '$(rm_out from-main)', expected main"
[ "$(rm_out from-branch)" = "branch-r1" ] || fail "from-branch ran '$(rm_out from-branch)', expected branch-r1"
[ "$(rm_out from-tag)" = "tag-v1" ] || fail "from-tag ran '$(rm_out from-tag)', expected tag-v1"
pass "each step ran its own ref's files (main / release/1 / v1.0.0)"

RM_BRANCH_REF=$(echo "$RM_DETAIL" | jq -r '.steps[] | select(.step_name=="from-branch") | .action_ref')
RM_BRANCH_REV=$(echo "$RM_DETAIL" | jq -r '.steps[] | select(.step_name=="from-branch") | .action_revision')
RM_TAG_REV=$(echo "$RM_DETAIL" | jq -r '.steps[] | select(.step_name=="from-tag") | .action_revision')
[ "$RM_BRANCH_REF" = "release/1" ] || fail "from-branch action_ref is '$RM_BRANCH_REF'"
[ "$RM_BRANCH_REV" = "$REFS_R1_SHA" ] || fail "from-branch pinned $RM_BRANCH_REV, expected $REFS_R1_SHA"
[ "$RM_TAG_REV" = "$REFS_TAG_SHA" ] || fail "from-tag pinned $RM_TAG_REV, expected $REFS_TAG_SHA (peeled tag)"
pass "steps are stamped with the resolved commits"

RM_CHILD_ID=$(echo "$RM_DETAIL" | jq -r '.steps[] | select(.step_name=="child") | .child_jobs[0].id')
RM_CHILD=$(acurl "$BASE_URL/api/jobs/$RM_CHILD_ID")
[ "$(echo "$RM_CHILD" | jq -r '.ref')" = "release/1" ] || { echo "$RM_CHILD" | jq .; fail "child job.ref is not release/1"; }
[ "$(echo "$RM_CHILD" | jq -r '.revision')" = "$REFS_R1_SHA" ] || fail "child revision is not release/1's tip"
[ "$(echo "$RM_CHILD" | jq -r '.steps[0].output.version')" = "branch-r1" ] || fail "child did not run release/1's files"
pass "type: task + ref: child job pinned to release/1 ($REFS_R1_SHA)"
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the guide**

Create `docs/src/content/docs/guides/git-refs.md`:

````markdown
---
title: Git Refs
description: Run an action or task from a specific branch, tag or commit of a git workspace
---

A reference to an action or a task can carry a `ref:` — a **branch**, a
**tag** or a full **commit SHA** of the git workspace that owns it. The
target then runs from that commit: its YAML (flow, inputs, action
definitions, secrets, connections) and its files. The typical use is several
releases of one workspace running side by side, with the default branch as
the manifest that pins which release each step, task or trigger runs.

```yaml
# on main
flow:
  import:
    action: import            # own workspace @ release/2.3
    ref: release/2.3
  export:
    action: billing.export    # workspace `billing` @ tag v4.1.0
    ref: v4.1.0

actions:
  nightly-2-3:
    type: task
    task: nightly             # own task @ commit
    ref: 3f2a9c0e1b2c3d4e5f60718293a4b5c6d7e8f901

triggers:
  nightly-billing:
    type: scheduler
    cron: "0 2 * * *"
    task: billing.nightly     # a trigger may now name another workspace's task
    ref: v4.1.0
```

Cutting a release is a pull request to `main` that bumps a `ref:`.

## Where `ref` is allowed

| Place | `ref` qualifies |
|---|---|
| Flow step `action:` | the action |
| `type: task` action `task:` | the task |
| Scheduler and webhook trigger `task:` | the task |

`ref:` on a hook, an event-source trigger, an agent action or an agent
`tools: [{task: …}]` entry is **rejected** with an error ("`ref` is not
supported on … yet"), never silently ignored. `ref:` on any action type other
than `task` is a validation error. A ref is a fixed string — it is never
templated.

## Ref forms

| Written | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 40 hex characters | that commit |
| `refs/heads/<name>` | branch `<name>` |
| `refs/tags/<name>` | tag `<name>` (an annotated tag resolves to its commit) |
| any other `<name>` | branch `<name>` if it exists, else tag `<name>` |

Short SHAs are not supported; a short hex string that names no branch or tag
fails with a hint to use the full 40-character SHA.

## How a reference resolves

The **owner** of the referenced name is decided first, then the name is
looked up in the owner's config **at the ref**:

| Written | Resolves to |
|---|---|
| unqualified name + `ref` | your own workspace at the ref |
| `ws.name` + `ref` | workspace `ws` at the ref |
| unqualified name, no `ref`, inside a pinned config | the same commit (the pin is inherited) |
| `ws.name`, no `ref`, inside a pinned config | `ws`'s live config (today's cross-workspace rule) |
| a library item + `ref` | error — libraries are server-level and have no ref |
| `ref` on a folder workspace | error — only git workspaces have refs |

The name does not have to exist on your default branch: a step may call an
action that exists only on the release branch. Inside a release, an
unqualified name resolves inside that release — `nightly` at `release/2.3`
calling `action: helper` gets 2.3's `helper`.

A ref'd target runs **in the owner's context at that commit**: its files,
its `secrets:`, its connections. Access control and the `shared: true`
connection gate key on the **workspace name**, never the ref:
`own@release/2.3` is your own workspace; `billing@v4.1.0` is cross-workspace,
exactly like `billing` (see [Cross-Workspace References](/guides/cross-workspace-references/)).

## Pinning

Every ref in a job resolves to a commit **once**, when that job is created:

- a flow step's action and a `type: task` step's task are pinned when the
  job containing them is created;
- a trigger resolves its ref when it fires;
- a child job resolves its own nested refs when it is created.

A job that runs on a ref (a ref'd `type: task` child, or a job created by a
trigger with `ref:`) is a **pinned job**: its flow, inputs, hooks, retries and
approvals all come from that one commit for its whole life, even if the
branch moves meanwhile. `{{ job.revision }}` is that commit and
`{{ job.ref }}` the ref as written (empty for an unpinned job); hooks see
`hook.ref`. A task-level retry, a hook job and a `type: task` child without
its own `ref` of a pinned job run on the same commit. **Re-run** and
**Restart** of a pinned job re-resolve its ref — a branch moves to its
current tip.

Jobs that use no `ref` keep today's behaviour.

## Freshness

A branch is re-checked only when a reference to it is resolved, at most
every `poll_interval_secs` of the owner workspace (the same freshness as the
default branch):

| Situation | What happens |
|---|---|
| Push to `release/2.3`, next run 2 minutes later | the new commit |
| Push, next run 20 seconds later | may still get the previous commit |
| A job is running when the push lands | keeps its commit |
| The branch is deleted | the next resolution fails (400 / trigger MISSED) |
| The git server is unreachable | the last-known commit, with a warning; none known → error |

Nothing is loaded ahead of time: the first run after a push — and the first
run after every server restart — fetches, checks out and loads that commit,
which can take seconds for a large repository.

## Task state

A pinned job's [task state](/guides/task-state/) and global state are kept
**per ref string**: `release/2.3` and `release/2.4` never overwrite each
other, and bumping `ref: v2.3.1` → `ref: v2.3.2` starts with empty state.

## Errors

| Condition | Execute API | Trigger | When a step is claimed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unknown workspace, folder owner, library item + `ref`, invalid ref | 400 | MISSED | — |
| Branch / tag not found, commit not found | 400 | MISSED | step fails |
| Name missing at that commit (`has no action` / `has no task … at ref`) | 400 | MISSED | step fails |
| The YAML at that commit does not load | 400 | MISSED | step fails |
| Git unreachable with nothing cached, load timeout, `sops`/`vals` failure | 500 | MISSED | step waits and retries (up to ~5 minutes), then fails |
| `ref` on an agent action | 400 | MISSED | — |

Rendering errors of a cross-workspace owner's templates are withheld from the
caller (a fixed "details withheld" message; the full error is in the server
log). Job detail, webhook responses, MCP job status and worker detail mask the
secret values of every commit a job touches; if those cannot be loaded they
answer 503 rather than risk showing them.

## Security

There is no allow-list: anyone who can merge a `ref:` into **any** workspace
can make the server run **any** branch, tag or commit of **any** configured
git workspace with that workspace's secrets — including resolving whatever
`vals` references an unreviewed branch contains. Restrict who can merge to
your workspaces' default branches accordingly.

## Upgrading

**Do not merge YAML that uses `ref:` until every server replica runs a
release with this feature.** An older replica ignores the unknown `ref:` key
and silently runs the default branch instead.

## Not yet supported

- A per-run ref override (testing a feature branch from the UI / API / CLI).
- `ref` on hooks, event sources, agent actions and agent task tools.
- Re-run and Restart of a pinned job whose task does not exist on the default
  branch are available through the API only; the UI hides them.
````

  ````markdown
  ### `pin_store`

  Pinned commits for [git refs](/guides/git-refs/). All fields are optional.

  ```yaml
  pin_store:
    dir: /var/lib/stroem/pins        # default: <temp>/stroem/pins
    keep_recent_per_workspace: 5     # default 5
  ```

  | Field | Default | Meaning |
  |---|---|---|
  | `dir` | `<temp>/stroem/pins` | One bare repository per git workspace plus one checkout per pinned commit. Must be private to ONE server process — startup fails if another process holds its lock. |
  | `keep_recent_per_workspace` | `5` | Recently used pins kept beyond those active jobs still need. |

  The bare repositories are never garbage-collected; on a container restart
  the default directory is empty again and refilled on demand.
  ````

Add a new section after `### Cross-Workspace References`:

```markdown
### Git refs (pinned references)
- Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-git-refs-design.md`. Flow-step `action:`, `type: task` `task:` and scheduler/webhook trigger `task:` take `ref:` (branch | tag | full SHA). Rust field `git_ref` (`#[serde(rename = "ref")]`); DB columns `job.git_ref`, `job.task_folder`, `job_step.{action_ref, task_workspace, task_ref, task_revision, pin_releases}`, `task_state.git_ref`, `workspace_state.git_ref` (migrations 049 + 050).
- `ref` on hooks / event sources / agent task tools / agent actions is REJECTED, never ignored: workflow models have no `deny_unknown_fields`, so those structs carry `git_ref` only to fail on it.
- Resolution: owner decided as for cross-workspace refs, then the name is looked up in owner@ref; with `ref` the owner rule is syntactic (`refs::ref_owner`). An unqualified, unref'd name inside a pinned config inherits the pin. Every ref in a job resolves to a commit once, at that job's creation (steps stamp `action_*` / `task_*`; dispatch reads ONLY `task_*`, never infers from the parent).
- `PinStore` (`workspace/pins.rs`): own bare repo per git workspace (never the watcher's clone), immutable `trees/{sha}` checkouts, configs cached by `(ws, commit)`, single-flight, `File::try_lock` on `{dir}/.lock`, lease-aware eviction (replica-local keep-set, every replica). `resolve` is lazy (listing TTL = `poll_interval_secs`) and adopts the fetched tip.
- One lookup: `WorkspaceManager::config_for(ws, Option<&PinRef>)` — `None` is today's live config. A pinned job (`git_ref IS NOT NULL`) reads its pin everywhere (`Settlement::resolve`, claim, dispatch, agent task tool, job detail). Connection resolution is role-scoped (`RoleScope`: caller / action owner / task owner / others) so two commits of one workspace never shadow each other.
- Claim: a TRANSIENT pin failure releases the step back to `ready` (`JobStepRepo::release_claim`, job row `FOR SHARE`, outcomes Released / Cancelled / CapReached / NotApplied, `MAX_PIN_RELEASES` = 30). Recovery's running-step failures pass `expected_claim` to `fail_or_retry`.
- Redaction: every job-scoped outlet (job detail — whole response incl. `approval_message` —, sync + polled webhook output, MCP `get_job_status`, worker detail per row, `fail_claimed_step`, `fail_task_step`) uses the per-job set (live values + every referenced pin's `secret_values`) and FAILS CLOSED (503 / masked row). Owner-side claim render errors across a workspace boundary are withheld, like dispatch.
- Re-run / Restart of a pinned source re-resolve its ref before any task lookup (`web/api/pinned_source.rs`).
- Rollout: never merge YAML using `ref:` before every replica runs the release — an old replica drops the key and runs the default branch.
```

In `### ACL (Access Control)`, add:

```markdown
- A job's ACL path: unpinned → live task folder; pinned → `job.task_folder` (never the live config). Any new read path that exposes a job must authorise with `check_job_acl(&JobRow)` / `acl::job_task_path`, never by looking up a task's folder in the live config. Lists filter in SQL with live pairs + pinned triples (`JobAclScope`) before ORDER/LIMIT — REST and MCP alike.
```

Then these one-line additions:
- **`### Settlement`**, end of the module-layout paragraph: `Settlement::resolve` reads `config_for(job.workspace, pin of job)` — a pinned job's cascade, hooks, retry and approvals all see one commit.
- **`### Cross-Workspace References`**, first bullet: "A reference may also carry `ref:` — see § Git refs."
- **`### Database`**: "Migration 050 builds its indexes inside the migration transaction; its header documents the manual `CONCURRENTLY` pre-run (migration 009 precedent)."
- **`### Worker Recovery`**: "Stale-worker and step-timeout failures pass the observed claim identity (`expected_claim`) so a released and reclaimed step is not failed on the previous attempt."
- **`### Task Duration Stats`**: "Pinned jobs are excluded (`git_ref IS NULL`)."

**CONTEXT.md.** Add after the "Provenance bucket" entry:

```markdown
- **Pin** — a resolved `(workspace, ref, commit)`: the ref as written plus the 40-hex commit it resolved to when the job containing the reference was created. Stored on a job (`git_ref` + `revision`) or a step (`action_ref` + `action_revision`, `task_*`).
- **Pinned job** — a job with `git_ref IS NOT NULL`: created in owner@ref (a ref'd `type: task` child, a ref'd trigger's job, or a hook / retry / re-run / restart of a pinned job). Every definition it reads comes from its one commit; its ACL folder is `job.task_folder`; its state is partitioned by the ref string.
- **PinStore** — the replica-local store of pinned commits (`workspace/pins.rs`): a bare repo per git workspace, immutable per-commit checkouts and configs. Separate from the default-branch watcher.
```

**docs/internal/TODO.md.** Add a section before `## Bugs Found & Fixed`:

```markdown
## Git refs follow-ups (spec 2026-10-02 § 16)

- [ ] Per-run ref override (API / UI / MCP / CLI) — the feature-branch testing case.
- [ ] Ref allow-list / trust gate per owner workspace (+ "a SHA must be reachable from an allowed ref"). Today anyone who can merge a `ref:` anywhere can run any ref of any git workspace with its secrets (accepted risk, spec § 12).
- [ ] Warm-up on publish (resolve + load a config's static refs in the background) and/or a background branch tracker; today the first pinned run after a push or restart pays fetch + checkout + load.
- [ ] `ref` on hooks (incl. a hook calling a ref'd `type: task` action), event sources, agent task tools and agent actions (agent MCP / task tools must be built from the action owner's pinned config — same work as cross-workspace agent actions).
- [ ] Ref-partitioned manual state upload (`POST …/state?ref=`).
- [ ] UI Re-run / Restart of a pinned job whose task does not exist on the default branch (the task page reads the live config).
- [ ] Pinning definitions for UNPINNED jobs (today's live-config drift).
- [ ] Libraries at a ref; bare-repo `git gc` in the PinStore; sharing pins across replicas.
- [ ] Pre-existing: the tarball-cache cleanup runs on the leader only (`recovery.rs:441`) while the cache is replica-local — followers never clean.
- [ ] Pre-existing: `fail_or_retry` with an empty expected list accepts any status, so a recovery sweep can race a worker's completion (only the release path and `fail_claimed_step` are guarded).
- [ ] Pre-existing: cancellation is two non-atomic calls (`JobRepo::cancel`, then `cancel_pending_steps`) and the claim SQL does not check the job's status — a worker can claim a `ready` sibling in between.
- [ ] Pre-existing: job log lines are never value-redacted on read (REST, WebSocket, MCP `get_job_logs`).
- [ ] Pre-existing: job detail `child_jobs[]` shows a child's id / workspace / task / status under the PARENT's ACL (metadata only).
```
